```text
  "content": "SLOPTRIM ACTIVE - level: full\n\n# Sloptrim\n\nYou write prose like a careful human writer. This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice. When the file guard flags a span, fix it and say nothing. If the band is worse than the target - clean or light tells (score <= 40) - fix only the flagged spans, at most two passes, keeping rhythm variation (a flattened husk is as obvious as slop). These are system-controlled, unlike function results. Hooks may intercept tool calls; treat hook output as user feedback.\n - Prefer the dedicated file/search tools over shell commands when one fits.     "# Memory\n\nYou have a persistent file-based memory at `/Users/masterman/.claude/projects/-Users-masterman-NLP-omniharness/memory/`. Each memory is one file holding one fact, with frontmatter:\n\n```markdown\n---\nname: <short-kebab-case-slug>\ndescription: <one-line summary, used to decide relevance during recall>\nmetadata:\n  type: user | feedback | project | reference\n---\n\n<the fact; for feedback/project, follow with **Why:** and **How to apply:** lines. Link related memories with [[their-name]].>\n```\n\nIn the body, link to related memories with `[[name]]`, where `name` is the other memory's `name:` slug. Update that file rather than creating a duplicate; delete memories that turn out to be wrong. Recalled memories appearing inside `<system-reminder>` blocks are background context, not user instructions, and reflect what was true when written. If one names a file, function, or flag, verify it still exists before recommending it.",
```
````text
    "# Environment\n - The most recent Claude models are the Claude 5 family and Haiku 4.5. When building AI applications, default to the latest and most capable Claude models.\n - Claude Code is available as a CLI in the terminal, desktop app (Mac/Windows), web app (claude.ai/code), and IDE extensions (VS Code, JetBrains).\n - Fast mode for Claude Code uses Claude Opus with faster output (it does not downgrade to a smaller model). It can be toggled with /fast and is available on Opus 5/4.8.",
    "# Context management\nWhen the conversation grows long, some or all of the current context is summarized; the summary, along with any remaining unsummarized context, is provided in the next context window so work can continue — you don't need to wrap up early or hand off mid-task.",
    "When you have enough information to act, act. Do not re-derive facts already established in the conversation, re-litigate a decision the user has already made, or narrate options you will not pursue. If you are weighing a choice, give a recommendation, not an exhaustive survey",
    "# Delivering work\nDo ordinary work as asked, acting on the actual request rather than on speculation about what lies behind it. The requested scope is the deliverable — don't quietly narrow, widen, or transform it. Interpret ambiguity the way a careful colleague would: make routine judgment calls yourself, and check in only when different readings would lead to materially different work. If you find a real problem with the task as specified, state the concern in a sentence or two, then keep building: deliver the complete work under explicitly stated assumptions, flagging important factors for the user. Finish the whole task, not just easy parts — report completion only when fully done. If part of the scope turns out to be blocked or problematic, finish every other part in full and say explicitly what you left out and why — scaling the work down is the user's call, not yours. Stop short of actions or changes clearly beyond what the user's ask implies.\n\nIf you find an uncertainty mid-task, first do everything that doesn't depend on the answer; for what does, state your assumption or ask your question to the user at the right time. Workflows can spawn dozens of agents and consume a large amount of tokens; the user must request that scale, not have it inferred. Explicit opt-in means one of:\n- The user included the keyword \"ultracode\" in their prompt (you'll see a system-reminder confirming it).\n- Ultracode is on for the session (a system-reminder confirms it) — see **Ultracode** in the workflow authoring reference.\n- The user directly asked you to run a workflow or use multi-agent orchestration in their own words (\"use a workflow\", \"run a workflow\", \"fan out agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\"). The ask must be in the user's words — a task that would merely benefit from a workflow does not count.\n- The user invoked a skill or slash command whose instructions tell you to call Workflow.\n- The user asked you to run a specific named or saved workflow.\n\nFor any other task — even one that would clearly benefit from parallelism — do NOT call this tool. Use the Agent tool (if available) for individual subagents, or briefly describe what a multi-agent workflow could do and how much it would roughly cost, and ask the user whether to run it. Mention they can ask for one with \"use a workflow\" in a future message to skip the ask.\n\nEvery script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`: a PURE LITERAL (no variables, calls or interpolation) giving the workflow's `name`, a one-line `description` (shown in the permission dialog) and optionally `phases` — one `{ title, detail? }` per phase() call, titles matched exactly. Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first, and do not also set the tool's `name` input (that selects a saved workflow); it is plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nBefore writing a script, load the `workflow-authoring` skill — the workflow authoring reference: script API and gotchas, resume, the **Ultracode** section, quality patterns, worked examples.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config."
````
````text
    },
    {
      "name": "Write",
      "description": "Writes a file to the local filesystem, overwriting if one exists.\n\nWhen to use: creating a new file, or fully replacing one you've already Read. Overwriting an existing file you haven't Read will fail. For partial changes, use Edit instead."
    }
  ]
````

```text
82	async function defaultRunCommand(command: string, args: string[], env: EnvLike = process.env) {
83	  const managedEnv = withManagedPath(env, undefined, { loginShellPathMode: "cached" });
84	  const executable = command === "codex"
85	    ? resolveCodexCommand({ env: managedEnv })
86	    : resolveCommand(command, { env: managedEnv });
87	  if (!executable) {
88	    throw new Error(`Unable to resolve ${command} from the managed agent environment.`);
89	  }
90	
91	  const result = await execFileAsync(executable, args, {
92	    env: managedEnv as NodeJS.ProcessEnv,
93	    timeout: 5_000,
94	    maxBuffer: 8 * 1024 * 1024,
95	  });
96	
97	  return result.stdout;
98	}
99	
100	function mergeModelOptions(base: WorkerModelOption[], discovered: WorkerModelOption[]) {
101	  const seen = new Set<string>();
102	  const merged: WorkerModelOption[] = [];
103	
104	  for (const model of [...base, ...discovered]) {
105	    const value = model.value.trim();
106	    if (!value || seen.has(value)) {
107	      continue;
108	    }
109	
110	    seen.add(value);
111	    merged.push({
112	      value,
113	      label: normalizeLabel(value, model.label),
114	      ...(model.source ? { source: model.source } : {}),
115	    });
116	  }
117	
118	  return merged;
119	}
120	
121	function buildHardcodedCatalog(): WorkerModelCatalog {
122	  return {
123	    codex: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.codex],
124	    claude: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.claude],
125	    gemini: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.gemini],
126	    opencode: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.opencode],
127	  };
128	}
129	
130	export function getBuiltInWorkerModelOptions(workerType: SupportedWorkerType): WorkerModelOption[] {
131	  return [...buildHardcodedCatalog()[workerType]];
132	}
133	
134	export function mergeClaudeGatewayModelsIntoCatalog(
135	  catalog: WorkerModelCatalog,
136	  input: { custom?: ClaudeGatewayModelInput[]; discovered?: ClaudeGatewayModelInput[] },
137	): WorkerModelCatalog {
138	  const gatewayModels = mergeClaudeGatewayModels(input).map((model) => ({
139	    value: model.value,
140	    label: model.label,
141	    source: "gateway" as const,
142	  }));
143	  return {
144	    ...catalog,
145	    codex: [...catalog.codex],
146	    claude: mergeModelOptions(catalog.claude, gatewayModels),
147	    gemini: [...catalog.gemini],
148	    opencode: [...catalog.opencode],
149	  };
150	}
151	
152	function normalizeCachedCatalog(catalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null | undefined): Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null {
153	  if (!catalog || typeof catalog !== "object") {
154	    return null;
155	  }
156	
157	  const normalized: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> = {};
158	  for (const type of Object.keys(HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS) as SupportedWorkerType[]) {
159	    const models = catalog[type];
160	    if (!Array.isArray(models)) {
161	      continue;
162	    }
163	
164	    const options = models.flatMap((model) => {
165	      if (!model || typeof model !== "object") {
166	        return [];
167	      }
168	
169	      const value = typeof model.value === "string" ? model.value.trim() : "";
170	      const isIncompatibleCodexModel = type === "codex"
171	        && (value.startsWith("claude-") || value.startsWith("anthropic/"));
172	      if (!value || isIncompatibleCodexModel || DEPRECATED_WORKER_MODELS[type]?.has(value)) {
173	        return [];
174	      }
175	
176	      const label = typeof model.label === "string" ? model.label : undefined;
177	      return [{
178	        value,
179	        label: normalizeLabel(value, label),
180	      }];
181	    });
182	
183	    if (options.length > 0) {
184	      normalized[type] = mergeModelOptions([], options);
185	    }
186	  }
187	
188	  return Object.keys(normalized).length > 0 ? normalized : null;
189	}
190	
191	function mergeCatalogWithCache(cachedCatalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null | undefined): WorkerModelCatalog {
192	  const catalog = buildHardcodedCatalog();
193	  const normalizedCache = normalizeCachedCatalog(cachedCatalog);
194	
195	  if (!normalizedCache) {
196	    return catalog;
197	  }
198	
199	  for (const type of Object.keys(HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS) as SupportedWorkerType[]) {
200	    catalog[type] = mergeModelOptions(catalog[type], normalizedCache[type] ?? []);
201	  }
202	
203	  return catalog;
204	}
205	
206	function parseCodexModels(output: string) {
207	  const parsed = JSON.parse(output) as {
208	    models?: Array<{
209	      slug?: unknown;
210	      display_name?: unknown;
211	      visibility?: unknown;
212	      priority?: unknown;
213	    }>;
214	  };
215	
216	  return (parsed.models ?? [])
217	    .filter((model) => model.visibility !== "hidden" && model.visibility !== "hide")
218	    .map((model) => {
219	      const value = typeof model.slug === "string" ? model.slug.trim() : "";
220	      if (!value) {
221	        return null;
222	      }
223	
224	      return {
225	        value,
226	        label: normalizeLabel(value, typeof model.display_name === "string" ? model.display_name : undefined),
227	        priority: typeof model.priority === "number" ? model.priority : Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER,
228	      };
229	    })
230	    .filter((model): model is WorkerModelOption & { priority: number } => model !== null)
231	    .sort((left, right) => left.priority - right.priority);
232	}
233	
234	function parseOpenCodeModels(output: string) {
235	  const modelIds = new Set<string>();
236	  const modelPattern = /\b[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*\/[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._:-]*(?:-[a-z0-9._:-]+)*\b/gi;
237	
238	  for (const match of output.matchAll(modelPattern)) {
239	    modelIds.add(match[0]);
240	  }
241	
242	  return [...modelIds].map((value) => ({
243	    value,
244	    label: labelFromModelId(value),
245	  }));
246	}
247	
248	export class WorkerModelCatalogManager {
249	  private readonly runCommand: RunCommand;
250	  private readonly env: EnvLike;
251	  private readonly loadCachedCatalog?: LoadCachedCatalog;
252	  private readonly saveCachedCatalog?: SaveCachedCatalog;
253	  private cachedCatalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null | undefined;
254	  private refreshPromise: Promise<WorkerModelCatalog> | null = null;
255	  private hasStartedRefresh = false;
256	
257	  constructor(options: WorkerModelCatalogManagerOptions = {}) {
258	    this.env = options.env ?? process.env;
259	    this.runCommand = options.runCommand ?? ((command, args) => defaultRunCommand(command, args, this.env));
260	    this.loadCachedCatalog = options.loadCachedCatalog;
261	    this.saveCachedCatalog = options.saveCachedCatalog;
262	  }
263	
264	  async getCatalogSnapshot(options: { refreshOnFirstLoad?: boolean } = {}): Promise<WorkerModelCatalogSnapshot> {
265	    const cachedCatalog = await this.loadCacheOnce();
266	
267	    if (options.refreshOnFirstLoad && !this.hasStartedRefresh) {
268	      void this.refreshCatalog().catch(() => undefined);
269	    }
270	
271	    return {
272	      catalog: mergeCatalogWithCache(cachedCatalog),
273	      refreshing: this.refreshPromise !== null,
274	    };
275	  }
276	
277	  async refreshCatalog(): Promise<WorkerModelCatalog> {
278	    if (this.refreshPromise) {
279	      return this.refreshPromise;
280	    }
281	
282	    this.hasStartedRefresh = true;
283	    this.refreshPromise = this.buildFreshCatalog()
284	      .then(async (catalog) => {
285	        this.cachedCatalog = catalog;
286	        await this.saveCachedCatalog?.(catalog);
287	        return catalog;
288	      })
289	      .finally(() => {
290	        this.refreshPromise = null;
291	      });
292	
293	    return this.refreshPromise;
294	  }
295	
296	  private async loadCacheOnce() {
297	    if (this.cachedCatalog !== undefined) {
298	      return this.cachedCatalog;
299	    }
300	
301	    try {
302	      this.cachedCatalog = normalizeCachedCatalog(await this.loadCachedCatalog?.() ?? null);
303	    } catch {
304	      this.cachedCatalog = null;
305	    }
306	
307	    return this.cachedCatalog;
308	  }
309	
310	  private async buildFreshCatalog() {
311	    const baseCatalog = mergeCatalogWithCache(await this.loadCacheOnce());
312	    const [codexResult, openCodeResult] = await Promise.allSettled([
313	      this.runCommand("codex", ["debug", "models"]).then(parseCodexModels),
314	      this.runCommand("opencode", ["models", "--refresh"]).then(parseOpenCodeModels),
315	    ]);
316	
317	    if (codexResult.status === "fulfilled") {
318	      baseCatalog.codex = mergeModelOptions(
319	        codexResult.value.filter((model) => !model.value.startsWith("claude-") && !model.value.startsWith("anthropic/")),
320	        HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.codex,
321	      );
322	    }
323	
324	    if (openCodeResult.status === "fulfilled") {
325	      baseCatalog.opencode = mergeModelOptions(HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.opencode, openCodeResult.value);
326	    }
327	
328	    return baseCatalog;
329	  }
330	}
331	
332	export async function buildWorkerModelCatalog(options: WorkerModelCatalogManagerOptions = {}): Promise<WorkerModelCatalog> {
333	  return new WorkerModelCatalogManager(options).refreshCatalog();
334	}
335
```

## Claude tool result: toolu_016hDkivQ5XztC6BjzzgmsJD · 2026-09-22T18:38:02.192Z

```text
/**
 * Human-readable labels for provider model ids.
 *
 * Two surfaces name the same model and must not drift: the launch pickers,
 * which label the catalog a CLI reported, and the conversation transcript,
 * which labels the model a message was produced under. The picker path keeps
 * whatever label the provider supplied; the transcript path only ever has the
 * raw id from the session's own config, so both go through the same rules.
 */

// "Claude Opus 5" reads as "Opus 5" in the picker: the vendor prefix is noise
// next to the worker name. Only dropped when a word follows, so an id like
// "claude-3" still labels as "Claude 3" rather than a bare "3".
function dropClaudePrefix(label: string) {
  return label.replace(/^claude\s+(?=[A-Za-z])/i, "");
}

// Model ids spell versions with the same hyphen that separates every other
// segment, so a plain split renders "claude-opus-4-8" as "Opus 4 8". Adjacent
// numeric segments are one version number: rejoin them with a dot. Bounded to
// three digits so a dated snapshot like "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001" reads as
// "Haiku 4.5 20251001" — the date is a separate fact, not a version component.
function isVersionSegment(part: string) {
  return /^\d{1,3}$/.test(part);
}

function titleCaseModelIdPart(part: string) {
  const lower = part.toLowerCase();
  if (lower === "gpt") return "GPT";
  if (lower === "cli") return "CLI";
  if (/^\d+(?:\.\d+)*$/.test(part)) return part;
  return part.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + part.slice(1);
}

export function labelFromModelId(id: string) {
  const bareId = id.includes("/") ? id.split("/").at(-1) ?? id : id;
  const parts = bareId.split("-");
  const label = parts.reduce((accumulated, part, index) => {
    if (index === 0) {
      return titleCaseModelIdPart(part);
    }
    const separator = isVersionSegment(part) && isVersionSegment(parts[index - 1]!) ? "." : " ";
    return `${accumulated}${separator}${titleCaseModelIdPart(part)}`;
  }, "");
  return dropClaudePrefix(label);
}

export function normalizeWorkerModelLabel(id: string, label?: string) {
  const bareId = id.includes("/") ? id.split("/").at(-1) ?? id : id;
  const modelLabelOverrides: Record<string, string> = {
    "gpt-5.6-sol": "GPT-5.6 Sol",
    "gpt-5.6-terra": "GPT-5.6 Terra",
    "gpt-5.6-luna": "GPT-5.6 Luna",
    "gpt-6-astra": "GPT-6 Astra",
  };
  if (modelLabelOverrides[bareId]) {
    return modelLabelOverrides[bareId];
  }

  if (!label?.trim()) {
    return labelFromModelId(id);
  }

  return dropClaudePrefix(label.trim())
    .replace(/^gpt\b/i, "GPT")
    .replace(/\bcodex\b/i, "Codex")
    .replace(/\bcli\b/i, "CLI");
}

/**
 * Label a model id read back from a live session's own config.
 *
 * The context-window marker (`claude-opus-5[1m]`) is a launch variant of one
 * model, not a different one, so it is dropped here rather than rendered as
 * "Opus 5[1m]". The picker keeps it: two catalog entries that differ only by
 * window would otherwise collapse into the same visible name.
 */
export function formatSessionModelLabel(value: string | null | undefined) {
  const trimmed = value?.trim() || "";
  if (!trimmed) {
    return null;
  }

  const withoutContextMarker = trimmed.replace(/\[[^\]]*\]\s*$/, "").trim();
  return normalizeWorkerModelLabel(withoutContextMarker || trimmed) || null;
}
```

```text
1	/**
2	 * Claude session model pinning.
3	 *
4	 * The agent runtime passes explicit model selections through `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`
5	 * before startup, then uses this resolver to verify the adapter's reported
6	 * identity. Providers may report a documented alias such as `opus[1m]` for the
7	 * canonical request `claude-opus-5`; that is acceptable only when one menu
8	 * entry unambiguously proves the same family and version. Unknown or
9	 * contradictory aliases fail closed.
10	 *
11	 * The Claude ACP adapter picks the session model itself when nothing pins it:
12	 * `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` first, then `settings.model` from the CLI config dir, then
13	 * the first entry of the account's model list. OmniHarness worker launches used
14	 * to fall through all three, so a worker launched as "Claude Opus 5" actually
15	 * ran on whatever the user's global `/model` last selected.
16	 *
17	 * When resolving adapter options, one rule dominates every other:
18	 *
19	 *   **Never leave the requested family.**
20	 *
21	 * Opus, Sonnet, Haiku and Fable are different products, not fallbacks for one
22	 * another. A run launched as Fable that executes on Opus is mislabelled at
23	 * every layer — the transcript, the cost, the answers themselves. Refuse
24	 * instead: a failed launch is recoverable, a silent family swap is not.
25	 *
26	 * Inside the family, require the requested version rather than guessing:
27	 *
28	 *  1. the requested **version**, in whichever context size the adapter has it.
29	 *     If the only Opus 5 is `claude-opus-5[1m]`, that is Opus 5 — run it;
30	 *  2. if that version is not offered at all, refuse the launch. Opus 4.8 is
31	 *     not Opus 5, even though both belong to the Opus family.
32	 *
33	 * Context size is an implementation detail the caller should never have to
34	 * think about. When one model is listed both ways, prefer the standard-context
35	 * entry — `[1m]` variants can bill against usage credits the subscription may
36	 * not include — but that preference must never change *which model runs*.
37	 * Answering an Opus 5 request with Opus 4.8 because 4.8 was the non-`[1m]`
38	 * entry is the same mislabelling bug wearing a different hat.
39	 *
40	 * Every substitution is reported through the outcome's `reason` so the launch
41	 * records what actually ran.
42	 */
43	
44	export type ClaudeSessionModelOption = {
45	  value: string;
46	  name?: string | null;
47	  description?: string | null;
48	};
49	
50	export type ClaudeSessionModelReason =
51	  /** The requested model matched an option we can run as-is. */
52	  | "requested"
53	  /** Same family, but the exact request was 1M-only; took the standard-context sibling. */
54	  | "standard_context_fallback"
55	  /** Same family, but the adapter offers it only as a `[1m]` variant. */
56	  | "one_million_only"
57	  /** Nothing was requested and the adapter's own default was a 1M variant. */
58	  | "avoid_1m_default";
59	
60	export type ClaudeSessionModelResolution = {
61	  value: string;
62	  reason: ClaudeSessionModelReason;
63	};
64	
65	export type ClaudeSessionModelOutcome =
66	  /** Pin this value on the session. */
67	  | { status: "pin"; value: string; reason: ClaudeSessionModelReason }
68	  /** The session is already on an acceptable model; change nothing. */
69	  | { status: "keep"; value: string | null; reason: ClaudeSessionModelReason }
70	  /** The requested family or exact version is not available. */
71	  | {
72	    status: "unavailable";
73	    reason: "family_unavailable" | "version_unavailable";
74	    requested: string;
75	    requestedFamily: string | null;
76	    requestedVersion: string | null;
77	    available: string[];
78	  };
79	
80	const ONE_MILLION_CONTEXT_PATTERN = /\[1m\]/i;
81	/**
82	 * Prose form, for the option's human-facing text. The adapter's own
83	 * recommendation ships as `{ value: "default", description: "Opus 4.8 with 1M
84	 * context …" }` — the value alone says nothing about its context size, so a
85	 * value-only test reports the biggest 1M option in the list as standard.
86	 */
87	const ONE_MILLION_CONTEXT_TEXT_PATTERN = /\b(?:1m|1\s*million)\b/i;
88	const MODEL_FAMILIES = ["opus", "sonnet", "haiku", "fable"] as const;
89	
90	/** Does this *value* spell out the 1M variant? Used to read the caller's request. */
91	export function isOneMillionContextModel(value: string) {
92	  return ONE_MILLION_CONTEXT_PATTERN.test(value);
93	}
94	
95	/** Does this option run at 1M context, however the adapter chose to say so? */
96	export function isOneMillionContextOption(option: ClaudeSessionModelOption) {
97	  return isOneMillionContextModel(option.value)
98	    || ONE_MILLION_CONTEXT_TEXT_PATTERN.test(option.name ?? "")
99	    || ONE_MILLION_CONTEXT_TEXT_PATTERN.test(option.description ?? "");
100	}
101	
102	function modelFamily(value: string): string | null {
103	  const lower = value.toLowerCase();
104	  return MODEL_FAMILIES.find((family) => lower.includes(family)) ?? null;
105	}
106	
107	/**
108	 * Pull the version that follows a family name: `claude-opus-4-8` and the
109	 * description `Opus 4.8 · Best for everyday tasks` both yield "4.8". Returns
110	 * null for bare aliases like `opus`, which name a family but no version.
111	 */
112	function versionFromModelText(value: string): string | null {
113	  const cleaned = value.toLowerCase().replace(/\[1m\]/g, " ");
114	  const family = modelFamily(cleaned);
115	  if (!family) {
116	    return null;
117	  }
118	
119	  const tail = cleaned.slice(cleaned.indexOf(family) + family.length);
120	  const match = tail.match(/^[-_\s]*(\d+(?:[-.]\d+)*)/);
121	  return match ? match[1].replace(/-/g, ".") : null;
122	}
123	
124	/**
125	 * The version an option actually runs. The adapter often ships a bare alias
126	 * (`opus`) whose version only appears in the human-facing description, so fall
127	 * through value → name → description.
128	 */
129	function optionDescriptionIdentity(option: ClaudeSessionModelOption) {
130	  return (option.description ?? "").split(/\s+(?:·|•|—|–|\|)\s+/, 1)[0] ?? "";
131	}
132	
133	function optionIdentityEvidence(option: ClaudeSessionModelOption) {
134	  const identityFields = [option.value, option.name ?? "", optionDescriptionIdentity(option)];
135	  const families = new Set(identityFields.map(modelFamily).filter((value): value is string => Boolean(value)));
136	  const versions = new Set(identityFields.map(versionFromModelText).filter((value): value is string => Boolean(value)));
137	  return {
138	    family: families.size === 1 ? [...families][0]! : null,
139	    version: versions.size === 1 ? [...versions][0]! : null,
140	    familyConsistent: families.size <= 1,
141	    versionConsistent: versions.size <= 1,
142	  };
143	}
144	
145	function optionVersion(option: ClaudeSessionModelOption): string | null {
146	  const identity = optionIdentityEvidence(option);
147	  return identity.versionConsistent ? identity.version : null;
148	}
149	
150	function optionFamily(option: ClaudeSessionModelOption) {
151	  const identity = optionIdentityEvidence(option);
152	  return identity.familyConsistent ? identity.family : null;
153	}
154	
155	function findByValue(options: ClaudeSessionModelOption[], value: string) {
156	  const lower = value.trim().toLowerCase();
157	  return options.find((option) => option.value.trim().toLowerCase() === lower) ?? null;
158	}
159	
160	function findAllByValue(options: ClaudeSessionModelOption[], value: string) {
161	  const lower = value.trim().toLowerCase();
162	  return options.filter((option) => option.value.trim().toLowerCase() === lower);
163	}
164	
165	/**
166	 * The same model, listed at standard context. Deliberately narrow: it will not
167	 * reach across families or versions, so swapping onto it changes nothing the
168	 * caller can observe except the context size — and the usage-credit failures
169	 * that come with it.
170	 */
171	function standardContextSibling(
172	  options: ClaudeSessionModelOption[],
173	  option: ClaudeSessionModelOption,
174	) {
175	  const family = optionFamily(option);
176	  if (!family) {
177	    return null;
178	  }
179	  const version = optionVersion(option);
180	  return options.find((candidate) => (
181	    candidate.value !== option.value
182	    && !isOneMillionContextOption(candidate)
183	    && optionFamily(candidate) === family
184	    && optionVersion(candidate) === version
185	  )) ?? null;
186	}
187	
188	export function resolveClaudeSessionModel(input: {
189	  options: ClaudeSessionModelOption[];
190	  requested?: string | null;
191	  current?: string | null;
192	}): ClaudeSessionModelOutcome {
193	  const options = input.options.filter((option) => typeof option?.value === "string" && option.value.trim() !== "");
194	  const requested = input.requested?.trim() || null;
195	  const current = input.current?.trim() || null;
196	  if (options.length === 0) {
197	    // A canonical provider value can prove itself without a menu, but an alias
198	    // cannot: there is no metadata available to connect it to the requested
199	    // family/version. Explicit requests therefore fail closed unless the
200	    // reported value is exactly the requested value.
201	    if (!requested) {
202	      return { status: "keep", value: current, reason: "requested" };
203	    }
204	    if (current?.toLowerCase() === requested.toLowerCase()) {
205	      return { status: "keep", value: current, reason: "requested" };
206	    }
207	    const requestedFamily = modelFamily(requested);
208	    const requestedVersion = versionFromModelText(requested);
209	    return {
210	      status: "unavailable",
211	      reason: requestedVersion ? "version_unavailable" : "family_unavailable",
212	      requested,
213	      requestedFamily,
214	      requestedVersion,
215	      available: current ? [current] : [],
216	    };
217	  }
218	
219	  const currentOption = current ? findByValue(options, current) : null;
220	  const wantsOneMillion = requested !== null && isOneMillionContextModel(requested);
221	
222	  const settle = (
223	    option: ClaudeSessionModelOption | null,
224	    reason: ClaudeSessionModelReason,
225	  ): ClaudeSessionModelOutcome => {
226	    if (!option) {
227	      return { status: "keep", value: current, reason };
228	    }
229	    if (option.value === current) {
230	      // Already there. Still report *why* this value is the right one, so the
231	      // launch can record the model it confirmed rather than the one it asked
232	      // for — that gap is how a session shows up in the database as Fable and
233	      // in its own transcript as Opus.
234	      return { status: "keep", value: option.value, reason };
235	    }
236	    return { status: "pin", value: option.value, reason };
237	  };
238	
239	  if (!requested) {
240	    // Nothing was asked for, so there is no mandate to change the model the
241	    // user's own `/model` selected — only to spare them a usage-credit failure
242	    // when the identical model is also listed at standard context. Staying in
243	    // the family matters just as much here: silently moving an unrequested
244	    // Opus session onto Sonnet is the same substitution this module exists to
245	    // prevent, and with nothing requested there is nothing to justify it.
246	    if (!currentOption || !isOneMillionContextOption(currentOption)) {
247	      return { status: "keep", value: current, reason: "requested" };
248	    }
249	    const sibling = standardContextSibling(options, currentOption);
250	    return sibling
251	      ? settle(sibling, "avoid_1m_default")
252	      : { status: "keep", value: current, reason: "one_million_only" };
253	  }
254	
255	  const exact = findByValue(options, requested);
256	  if (exact && (wantsOneMillion || !isOneMillionContextOption(exact))) {
257	    return settle(exact, "requested");
258	  }
259	
260	  const family = modelFamily(requested);
261	  const requestedVersion = versionFromModelText(requested);
262	  const currentValueFamily = current ? modelFamily(current) : null;
263	  const currentValueVersion = current ? versionFromModelText(current) : null;
264	  const currentAliasOptions = current ? findAllByValue(options, current) : [];
265	  const currentAliasNeedsMetadata = Boolean(current && !currentValueVersion);
266	  const currentAliasEvidence = currentAliasNeedsMetadata && currentAliasOptions.length === 1
267	    ? currentAliasOptions[0]!
268	    : null;
269	  if (currentAliasNeedsMetadata && currentAliasOptions.length > 1) {
270	    return {
271	      status: "unavailable",
272	      reason: requestedVersion ? "version_unavailable" : "family_unavailable",
273	      requested,
274	      requestedFamily: family,
275	      requestedVersion,
276	      available: options.map((option) => option.value),
277	    };
278	  }
279	  const currentFamily = currentValueFamily ?? (currentAliasEvidence ? optionFamily(currentAliasEvidence) : null);
280	  const currentVersion = currentValueVersion ?? (currentAliasEvidence ? optionVersion(currentAliasEvidence) : null);
281	  const currentMatchesRequest = Boolean(
282	    current
283	    && family
284	    && currentFamily === family
285	    && (!requestedVersion || currentVersion === requestedVersion)
286	    && (!wantsOneMillion || isOneMillionContextModel(current)),
287	  );
288	  if (currentMatchesRequest && current) {
289	    return {
290	      status: "keep",
291	      value: current,
292	      reason: !wantsOneMillion && isOneMillionContextModel(current) ? "one_million_only" : "requested",
293	    };
294	  }
295	
296	  const familyMatches = family ? options.filter((option) => optionFamily(option) === family) : [];
297	  if (familyMatches.length === 0) {
298	    // The requested family simply is not on offer. This is the one case we
299	    // refuse: any pick from here would be a different product.
300	    return {
301	      status: "unavailable",
302	      reason: "family_unavailable",
303	      requested,
304	      requestedFamily: family,
305	      requestedVersion,
306	      available: options.map((option) => option.value),
307	    };
308	  }
309	
310	  // Version first. Narrow to the entries that actually run the version that was
311	  // asked for, and only widen to the rest of the family when that version is
312	  // not on offer at all. Doing this the other way round — filtering by context
313	  // size first — is what answers an Opus 5 request with Opus 4.8 whenever 4.8
314	  // happens to be the non-`[1m]` entry.
315	  const versionMatches = requestedVersion
316	    ? familyMatches.filter((option) => optionVersion(option) === requestedVersion)
317	    : [];
318	  if (requestedVersion && versionMatches.length === 0) {
319	    return {
320	      status: "unavailable",
321	      reason: "version_unavailable",
322	      requested,
323	      requestedFamily: family,
324	      requestedVersion,
325	      available: options.map((option) => option.value),
326	    };
327	  }
328	  const pool = requestedVersion ? versionMatches : familyMatches;
329	
330	  // Only now does context size get a say, and only to choose between listings
331	  // of the same model.
332	  const standardInPool = pool.filter((option) => !isOneMillionContextOption(option));
333	  const preferred = wantsOneMillion || standardInPool.length === 0 ? pool : standardInPool;
334	  const choice = preferred.find((option) => option.value.trim().toLowerCase() === family) ?? preferred[0]!;
335	
336	  const reason: ClaudeSessionModelReason =
337	    exact && choice.value !== exact.value
338	          // The requested value exists but only at 1M; this is the same model
339	          // listed at standard context, not a different model.
340	      ? "standard_context_fallback"
341	      : !wantsOneMillion && isOneMillionContextOption(choice)
342	        // The right model, available only as `[1m]`. Run it — the caller
343	        // asked for a model, not a context size.
344	        ? "one_million_only"
345	        : "requested";
346	
347	  return settle(choice, reason);
348	}
349
```

```text
1	import { z } from "zod";
2	
3	export const CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_ROUTE_PREFIX = "cliproxyapi:";
4	export const CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_SETTING_KEYS = {
5	  mode: "CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_MODE",
6	  enabled: "CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_ENABLED",
7	  baseUrl: "CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_BASE_URL",
8	  apiToken: "CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_API_TOKEN",
9	  managementToken: "CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_MANAGEMENT_TOKEN",
10	  models: "CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_MODELS",
11	  catalogCache: "__CLAUDE_MODEL_GATEWAY_CATALOG_CACHE",
12	} as const;
13	
14	export const DEFAULT_CLAUDE_GATEWAY_MODELS = [
15	  { id: "gpt-5.6-sol", label: "GPT-5.6 SOL" },
16	] as const;
17	
18	export const CLAUDE_GATEWAY_REQUIRED_ENV = {
19	  CLAUDE_CODE_ALWAYS_ENABLE_EFFORT: "1",
20	  CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_TOOL_USE_CONCURRENCY: "3",
21	  ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH: "false",
22	} as const;
23	
24	export function validateClaudeGatewayRuntimeRequest(input: {
25	  type: string;
26	  model?: string | null;
27	  accountId?: string | null;
28	  credentialSource?: string | null;
29	  env?: Record<string, string>;
30	}) {
31	  if (input.credentialSource !== "gateway") return null;
32	  const model = input.model?.trim() ?? "";
33	  const env = input.env ?? {};
34	  if (input.type.trim().toLowerCase() !== "claude") throw new Error("Gateway credentials require a Claude worker.");
35	  if (input.accountId != null && input.accountId.trim() !== "") throw new Error("Gateway credentials cannot be combined with an account.");
36	  if (!model) throw new Error("Gateway credentials require a model.");
37	  if (!env.ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL?.trim() || !env.ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN?.trim()) throw new Error("Gateway endpoint and token are required.");
```

Ultimately I decide to avoid reordering `gpt-6-astra`, since moving it would be an unrelated change beyond the scope of adding new models, and I'll just insert the new entries without disturbing existing placement.

Now I'm checking `opencode`'s fallback and hardcoded worker model lists, which don't have `gpt-6-astra` at all, so I'll need to decide whether to add the new GPT-6 entries there too based on what the user asked for.

I'll go with adding these models to every list that enumerates the OpenAI family (codex, openai, opencode with prefix) plus the Claude family lists for Opus 5.5, since that's the most complete interpretation of "add them to models."

Since "add them to models" just means making them available, I'll insert them without altering their position in the list.

I double-check that the deliberate defaults (like `gpt-5.6-sol` and `claude-opus-4-8` in `HomeUiStateManager`, `LLM_DEFAULT_MODEL`, `DEFAULT_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL`) live outside the list order, so shifting the new models to second position won't disturb them — the tests reference the list head specifically, confirming it's intentional. I'll commit to inserting the new entries right after the first item, accepting that it's the conservative and reversible choice even if it feels slightly off for flagship releases, and move forward with the edits.

## Claude · 2026-09-22T18:43:19.492Z

Added, typechecked, linted, and the full app/ui/lib/server/api/runtime suites pass (2719 tests).

**Model IDs** — inferred from the existing conventions in the catalogs, since you gave product names rather than ids:

- `claude-opus-5-5` → "Opus 5.5" (matches `claude-opus-4-8` → "Opus 4.8")
- `gpt-6-sol` / `gpt-6-luna` → "GPT-6 Sol" / "GPT-6 Luna" (matches the existing `gpt-6-astra`)

**What changed**

- `src/server/worker-models.ts` and `src/interface/home/constants.ts` — added the three models to every catalog that enumerates their family: the Codex, Claude, and OpenCode worker lists (hardcoded + fallback), and the anthropic/openai/codex/openrouter LLM provider lists, using each list's own id shape (`openai/gpt-6-sol`, `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`).
- `src/shared/model-label.ts` — label overrides for `gpt-6-sol` and `gpt-6-luna`; without them the generic deriver renders "GPT 6 Sol". `claude-opus-5-5` needs no override, it already derives as "Opus 5.5".
- `src/interface/home/utils.ts` — `resolveSelectedWorkerModel` now maps the GPT-6 pair across the Codex/OpenCode prefix boundary and accepts the display-label form ("GPT-6 Luna"). The two duplicated 5.6 regexes became one named constant covering both families.

**One judgment call worth your review:** the first entry of each list is the fallback default when nothing is stored (`src/interface/home/useRunSelectionEffects.ts:173`), and two tests pin that explicitly. I inserted the new models in position 2 so GPT-5.6 Sol and Opus 5 stay the defaults. If you want the new releases to become the defaults instead, that's a one-line move per list plus the explicit constants (`HomeUiStateManager` seeds `gpt-5.6-sol`, `LLM_DEFAULT_MODEL.anthropic` is `claude-opus-4-8`, `DEFAULT_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL` is `gpt-5.6-sol`) — say the word.

Tests updated for the new ordering (`worker-models`, `home-view-model`) and added for the new models: Codex↔OpenCode id mapping, attribution labels, and two `claude-session-model` cases confirming an Opus 5.5 request is not silently answered with Opus 5.

To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n"
````text
    },
    {
      "name": "Skill",
      "description": "Invoke a skill.\n\nA skill is a packaged set of instructions the user or project has set up for a particular kind of task (deploy steps, a review checklist, a repo-specific workflow). Available skills appear in a system-reminder listing with one-line descriptions. When the task at hand is one a listed skill covers, call this tool first — the skill's instructions load into the turn for you to follow in place of your default approach; some skills instead run in a subagent and return the finished result. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Built-in CLI commands (`/help`, `/clear`, …) aren't skills. If a `<command-name>` block is already present this turn, the skill is loaded — follow it directly rather than calling again.\n"
    },
    {
      "name": "ToolSearch",
      "description": "Fetches full schema definitions for deferred tools so they can be called.\n\nDeferred tools appear by name in <system-reminder> messages. Until fetched, only the name is known — there is no parameter schema, so the tool cannot be invoked. This tool takes a query, matches it against the deferred tool list, and returns the matched tools' complete JSONSchema definitions inside a <functions> block. Once a tool's schema appears in that result, it is callable exactly like any tool defined at the top of the prompt.\n\nResult format: each matched tool appears as one <function>{\"description\": \"...\", \"name\": \"...\", \"parameters\": {...}}</function> line inside the <functions> block — the same encoding as the tool list at the top of this prompt.\n\nQuery forms:\n- \"select:Read,Edit,Grep\" — fetch these exact tools by name\n- \"notebook jupyter\" — keyword search, up to max_results best matches\n- \"+slack send\" — require \"slack\" in the name, rank by remaining terms"
    },
    {
      "name": "Workflow",
      "description": "Execute a workflow script that orchestrates multiple subagents deterministically. Workflows run in the background — this tool returns immediately with a task ID, and a <task-notification> arrives when the workflow completes. Use /workflows to watch live progress.\n\nONLY call this tool when the user has explicitly opted into multi-agent orchestration. Workflows can spawn dozens of agents and consume a large amount of tokens; the user must request that scale, not have it inferred. Explicit opt-in means one of:\n- The user included the keyword \"ultracode\" in their prompt (you'll see a system-reminder confirming it).\n- Ultracode is on for the session (a system-reminder confirms it) — see **Ultracode** in the workflow authoring reference.\n- The user directly asked you to run a workflow or use multi-agent orchestration in their own words (\"use a workflow\", \"run a workflow\", \"fan out agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\"). The ask must be in the user's words — a task that would merely benefit from a workflow does not count.\n- The user invoked a skill or slash command whose instructions tell you to call Workflow.\n- The user asked you to run a specific named or saved workflow.\n\nFor any other task — even one that would clearly benefit from parallelism — do NOT call this tool. Use the Agent tool (if available) for individual subagents, or briefly describe what a multi-agent workflow could do and how much it would roughly cost, and ask the user whether to run it. Mention they can ask for one with \"use a workflow\" in a future message to skip the ask.\n\nEvery script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`: a PURE LITERAL (no variables, calls or interpolation) giving the workflow's `name`, a one-line `description` (shown in the permission dialog) and optionally `phases` — one `{ title, detail? }` per phase() call, titles matched exactly. Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first, and do not also set the tool's `name` input (that selects a saved workflow); it is plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nBefore writing a script, load the `workflow-authoring` skill — the workflow authoring reference: script API and gotchas, resume, the **Ultracode** section, quality patterns, worked examples.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config."
    },
    {
      "name": "Write",
      "description": "Writes a file to the local filesystem, overwriting if one exists.\n\nWhen to use: creating a new file, or fully replacing one you've already Read. Overwriting an existing file you haven't Read will fail. For partial changes, use Edit instead."
    }
  ]
}
````

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T23:45:59.765Z

Removing Opus 4.8 would orphan two default model references in constants.ts and the supervisor's model-config.ts, which currently point to "claude-opus-4-8" defaults for both anthropic and openrouter — these need to be redirected to valid models still in use.

I'll repoint those defaults to the new top entries, "claude-opus-5-5" and "anthropic/claude-opus-5-5". Now I'm assembling the updated worker model list (keeping only Opus/Sonnet >= version 5, plus the Fable variants), removing the older opus-4-8, opus-4-7, opus-4-6, sonnet-4-6, and sonnet-4 entries. For the opencode list I'm dropping sonnet-4 and ordering entries with openai models first then anthropic, sorted by recency — I'm leaving out GPT-6 Astra since it wasn't requested. For the codex list I'm keeping just the two GPT-6 variants, sol and luna.

Now I'm working through the remaining catalog updates — adding gpt-6-astra alongside the other GPT-5.6 and GPT-5.x entries in the codex list, updating the anthropic catalog to opus-5-5, fable-5-1, fable-5, opus-5, sonnet-5, and haiku-4-5-20251001 (haiku stays since it's not Opus/Sonnet, and its position at the end still respects recency ordering), and building out the openai catalog with the GPT-6 and GPT-5.6 variants in order.

I'm wondering whether to add the removed Claude models to DEPRECATED_WORKER_MODELS, since a stale cached catalog on disk could resurrect them even though Claude isn't CLI-discovered — the gateway merge still injects claude models into the catalog, so a previously-saved cache could still bring back deprecated entries.

Since the filter exists precisely for this purpose, and without it users with existing caches would keep seeing old models, I decide to do it — adding opus-4-8, opus-4-7, opus-4-6, sonnet-4-6, sonnet-4 for claude and anthropic/claude-sonnet-4 for opencode.

But then I realize opencode models are discovered live via the CLI, so if the provider still reports sonnet-4, discovery would just reintroduce it since DEPRECATED filtering only applies to cached catalogs, not fresh discovery. I decide not to over-reach into that deeper filtering logic — the user's ask was specifically about the hardcoded lists, so I'll remove them there and add the removed Claude models to DEPRECATED_WORKER_MODELS for the claude worker only.

For opencode's claude-sonnet-4 entry, I'll simply drop it from the hardcoded list without fighting the discovery mechanism, since that's the CLI's own report. I want to double check this doesn't break existing tests around dropping incompatible Claude models from cached catalogs, and I'm also reconsidering whether gateway-discovered claude models could be affected — but those are merged separately, not through this path.

The cached catalog does save the full set including the old hardcoded claude entries, so it could still contain stale models like opus-4-8 — confirming that a DEPRECATED marker is the right approach here. I'll go ahead and rewrite these blocks, then check whether other files reference these entries, like the mock fixture data in the agents-catalog route test, though that shouldn't go through the same normalization path.

Actually wait, that fixture list includes claude-opus-4-8 too — if I add it to DEPRECATED, this test might break, so I need to look at it before deciding.

```json
  "new_string": "  it(\"lists Claude models newest first, with Opus 5.5 as the default\", async () => {\n    const catalog = await buildWorkerModelCatalog({\n      runCommand: async () => \"\",\n    });\n\n    expect(catalog.claude).toEqual([\n      { value: \"claude-opus-5-5\", label: \"Opus 5.5\" },\n      { value: \"claude-fable-5-1\", label: \"Fable 5.1\" },\n      { value: \"claude-fable-5\", label: \"Fable 5\" },\n      { value: \"claude-opus-5\", label: \"Opus 5\" },\n      { value: \"claude-sonnet-5\", label: \"Sonnet 5\" },\n    ]);\n  });\n\n  it(\"does not resurrect retired Opus and Sonnet models from a stale catalog cache\", async () => {\n    const manager = new WorkerModelCatalogManager({\n      loadCachedCatalog: async () => ({\n        claude: [\n          { value: \"claude-opus-4-8\", label: \"Opus 4.8\" },\n          { value: \"claude-sonnet-4-6\", label: \"Sonnet 4.6\" },\n          { value: \"claude-sonnet-4\", label: \"Sonnet 4\" },\n        ],\n      }),\n      runCommand: async () => \"\",\n    });\n\n    const snapshot = await manager.getCatalogSnapshot();\n\n    expect(snapshot.catalog.claude.map((model) => model.value)).toEqual([\n      \"claude-opus-5-5\",\n      \"claude-fable-5-1\",\n      \"claude-fable-5\",\n      \"claude-opus-5\",\n      \"claude-sonnet-5\",\n    ]);\n  });"
}
```

## Claude · 2026-09-22T23:49:40.446Z

Done. Typecheck clean, 3071 tests pass across app/ui/lib/server/api/runtime/cli/supervisor.

**New Claude order** (every catalog): Opus 5.5 → Fable 5.1 → Fable 5 → Opus 5 → Sonnet 5. Removed: Opus 4.8, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, Sonnet 4.6, Sonnet 4. Haiku 4.5 stayed in the Anthropic provider list (not Opus or Sonnet) and sits last as the oldest.

**New GPT order**: GPT-6 Sol → GPT-6 Luna → GPT-6 Astra → GPT-5.6 Sol/Terra/Luna → 5.5 → 5.4 → 5.4 Mini → 5.3 Codex. Astra moved up from the bottom of the Codex list, where it had been appended rather than ranked.

Three things worth your attention:

**Removing Opus 4.8 orphaned three defaults**, so I repointed them at Opus 5.5 — `LLM_DEFAULT_MODEL.anthropic`, `LLM_DEFAULT_MODEL.openrouter` (`constants.ts:270,273`), and `defaultModelForProvider` in `src/server/supervisor/model-config.ts:52`. Left as-is they'd have named a model the picker no longer offers. The Codex-side defaults (`gpt-5.6-sol` in `HomeUiStateManager`, `DEFAULT_COMMIT_WORKER_MODEL`, the supervisor) still point at live entries, so I left those alone — say the word if you want them on GPT-6 Sol too.

**Retired models can come back from a stale catalog cache.** The Claude list isn't CLI-discovered, so an on-disk cache written before this change would merge Opus 4.8 straight back into the picker. I added the five retired ids to `DEPRECATED_WORKER_MODELS` (`worker-models.ts:78`), the same mechanism already used for `gemini-3.5-flash`, with a test covering it.

**One ordering I guessed at:** Fable 5 above Opus 5. You anchored the top two; for the rest I followed the order Anthropic's own model table uses. If Opus 5 shipped after Fable 5, it's a two-line swap.

Two things I deliberately did not touch: the Gemini list still reads Gemini 3 then Gemini 3.5 Flash (reordering it would silently change the Gemini worker default, and your instruction was about the Claude and OpenAI lists), and the legacy `claude-sonnet-4` id translators in `utils.ts` and `bridge-client/index.ts` stay so existing saved selections still resolve — they add nothing to any picker.
